# MedVision consistency audit — `skills/` and `docsite/` vs. the codebase

Method: 23 parallel Opus auditors (12 skill units, 11 docsite units) read every file in their unit, extracted each concrete verifiable claim, and checked it against the working trees of `Github/MedVision` (master @ 780e247, dirty) and `verl` (medvision-rl @ cf575004). Every finding was then re-checked by an independent verifier instructed to *refute* it.

**Result: 200 raw findings → 152 confirmed, 43 adjusted, 5 refuted, 0 unverified.** 23/23 units returned.

Severity: high = a command/path/flag/number a user would copy and it fails or misleads; medium = misleading description of behaviour; low = minor drift or coverage gap.


---

## HIGH (12)

### H1. `docsite/source/benchmarking/llm-judge-parsing.md:147`  
*docsite:bench-judge · wrong-path · confirmed*

- **Claim:** ```bash python -m medvision_bm.benchmark.summarize_TL_task \ --task_dir Results/MedVision-TL-v2-CoT -p 32 --skip_model_wo_parsed_files \ --removed_samples_dir Data/Datasets \ --parsed_dirname llm-parsed --resps_key LLM_filtered_resps ```
- **Code says:** The judge never writes a bare `llm-parsed` directory. `judge_config.py:348` sets `LLM_PARSED_DIRNAME = "llm-parsed"` and `judge_config.py:451` builds the real name as `f"{LLM_PARSED_DIRNAME}{judge_suffix(judge)}"`; the only registered reader's `out_suffix` is `"_gemma-4-31b"` (judge_config.py:173), and the docstring at judge_config.py:447-449 states "Every registered reader has a non-empty suffix, so the bare `llm-parsed` is never produced." `apply_judge.py:588` (`out_dirname = llm_parsed_dirname(args.limit, args.judge)`) and `apply_judge.py:599` write into that directory, and the driver itself passes `PD="llm-parsed${JUDGE_SUFFIX}"` (run_llm_parsing.sh:684) to `--parsed_dirname` (run_llm_parsing.sh:725). Run as printed, `os.path.join(model_dir, "llm-parsed")` never exists, and with `--skip_model_wo_parsed_files` every model directory is skipped (summarize_TL_task.py:939-942), so the command exits 0 having summarized nothing.
- **Fix:** Use the reader-suffixed directory: `--parsed_dirname llm-parsed_gemma-4-31b` (generally `llm-parsed_<reader>`).

### H2. `docsite/source/benchmarking/llm-judge-parsing.md:118`  
*docsite:bench-judge · wrong-number · confirmed*

- **Claim:** | `TP` | GPUs one judge process spans (default per reader; 2 for gemma-4-31b) |
- **Code says:** run_llm_parsing.sh:109 sets `TP="${TP:-1}"` unconditionally BEFORE sourcing judge_env.sh, and judge_env.sh:66 adopts any pre-set TP (`if [ -n "${TP:-}" ]; then JUDGE_TP="${TP}"; fi`), so the registry value `"tensor_parallel": 2` (judge_config.py:172) is always overridden. The script's own comment at run_llm_parsing.sh:103-107 says "Single-GPU by default: one shard (NUM_SHARDS=1) spanning one card (TP=1), overriding the reader's registry TP", and script/llm-parsing/README.md:252 documents "`TP` | GPUs one process spans. Default 1 (single GPU, overriding the reader's registry value)". This was already the behaviour at the commit that added this docsite page (b46d00e: `TP="${TP:-1}"` at line 109).
- **Fix:** State the actual default: `TP` defaults to 1 (single GPU), overriding the reader's registry value of 2; raise it only to fit the weights.

### H3. `docsite/source/benchmarking/llm-judge-parsing.md:119`  
*docsite:bench-judge · wrong-number · confirmed*

- **Claim:** | `NUM_SHARDS` | independent processes (default: visible GPUs ÷ `TP`) |
- **Code says:** run_llm_parsing.sh:124 sets `NUM_SHARDS="${NUM_SHARDS:-1}"`; the devices/TP expression only appears as a dead fallback at run_llm_parsing.sh:580 (`local N="${NUM_SHARDS:-$(( ${#DEV[@]} / JUDGE_TP ))}"`, unreachable because NUM_SHARDS is already set). GPUs are only derived from a count when `GPU_NUM` is set (run_llm_parsing.sh:115-122). script/llm-parsing/README.md:253 documents "`NUM_SHARDS` | independent processes. Default 1".
- **Fix:** Say the default is 1, and document `GPU_NUM` (total GPUs; `NUM_SHARDS = GPU_NUM / TP`, must divide evenly) as the one-knob way to spread over more cards.

### H4. `docsite/source/reference/cli.md:53`  
*docsite:cli · wrong-path · confirmed*

- **Claim:** Six code blocks pass non-existent task-list files: `--tasks_list_json_path tasks_list/TL.json` (L53), `tasks_list/Detection.json` (L93), `tasks_list/AD.json` (L132), `--tasks_json tasks_list/TL.json` (L208), `--tasks_list_json_path_TL tasks_list/TL.json` (L368 SFT, L487 RFT).
- **Code says:** `ls tasks_list/` contains only OOD/, experimental/, README.md, tasks_MedVision-AD-CoT.json, tasks_MedVision-AD__train_SFT.json, tasks_MedVision-TL-CoT.json, tasks_MedVision-TL__train_SFT.json, tasks_MedVision-detect-CoT.json, tasks_MedVision-detect__train_SFT.json — there is no TL.json / AD.json / Detection.json. Real launchers use the long names: script/benchmark-TL/eval__Qwen-2.5-VL__TL.sh:24 `tasks_list_json_path="${benchmark_dir}/tasks_list/tasks_MedVision-TL-CoT.json"`. Every other docsite page also uses the real names: docsite/source/index.md:83 `tasks_list/tasks_MedVision-AD-CoT.json`, docsite/source/benchmarking/running-evaluations.md:51, docsite/source/fine-tuning/sft.md:110-112, docsite/source/fine-tuning/rft.md:25-27. Copy-pasting any of these six commands fails with FileNotFoundError.
- **Fix:** Replace with the shipped names: `tasks_list/tasks_MedVision-TL-CoT.json`, `tasks_list/tasks_MedVision-detect-CoT.json`, `tasks_list/tasks_MedVision-AD-CoT.json` for evaluation/download, and `tasks_list/tasks_MedVision-TL__train_SFT.json` for the SFT/RFT examples (L368, L487).

### H5. `docsite/source/getting-started/installation.md:99`  
*docsite:getting-started · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "`env_setup` ... In practice you rarely call it directly — the per-model launchers under `script/benchmark-*/` invoke it for you with the right arguments."
- **Code says:** No launcher anywhere under `script/` invokes `medvision_bm.benchmark.env_setup`: `grep -rn "benchmark.env_setup" script/` returns 0 hits (the only `env_setup` hits in `script/benchmark-*/` are the unrelated `--env_setup_only` debug flag, and `script/sft`+`script/rft` use the *different* module `medvision_bm.sft.env_setup`). The representative launcher script/benchmark-AD/eval__Qwen-2.5-VL__AD.sh:56-62 uses "Method 1": `python -m medvision_bm.benchmark.install_medvision_ds --data_dir ...`, `python -m medvision_bm.benchmark.install_vendored_lmms_eval --lmms_eval_opt_deps qwen2_5_vl`, `pip install -r requirements/requirements_eval_qwen25vl.txt --no-deps`, then passes `--skip_env_setup` to the eval driver. The commented-out "Method 2" path instead runs the driver's own inline installers (src/medvision_bm/benchmark/eval__qwen2_5_vl.py:297-312: ensure_hf_hub_installed / install_vendored_lmms_eval / install_medvision_ds / install_torch_cu124 / install_vllm / install_transformers_accelerate_for_qwen25vl) — still not the `env_setup` module. `benchmark.env_setup` is actually invoked only by the Dockerfiles (e.g. dockerfile/Dockerfile.eval_qwen25vl:46).
- **Fix:** Replace the last sentence with something like: "`env_setup` is what the published Docker images run at build time (`dockerfile/Dockerfile.eval_*`). The launchers under `script/benchmark-*/` instead run the equivalent steps by hand — `install_medvision_ds`, then `python -m medvision_bm.benchmark.install_vendored_lmms_eval --lmms_eval_opt_deps <extra>`, then `pip install -r requirements/requirements_eval_<model>.txt --no-deps` — and pass `--skip_env_setup` to the eval driver." Also add `install_vendored_lmms_eval` to this section, since it is a required standalone step in that flow and is currently never mentioned on the page.

### H6. `docsite/source/fine-tuning/sft.md:68`  
*docsite:sft · wrong-path · confirmed*

- **Claim:** The prepared dataset lands in `--prepared_ds_dir`, defaulting to a path derived from the per-task limits, e.g. `<data_dir>/tmp_prepared_ds_AD5500_D110000_TL5500_all121000`.
- **Code says:** src/medvision_bm/sft/train__SFT-CoT__qwen2_5_vl.py:191-201 builds the default as `os.path.join(data_dir, "SFT-CoT_datasets", model_family_name, f"ds__AD{...}_D{...}_TL{...}_all{...}" + ds_dir_suffix)` where `ds_dir_suffix = f"__resized-wh-{new_shape_hw[1]}x{new_shape_hw[0]}"` (line 132) or `"__original"` (line 134). The tokens come from `_true_size()` (line 75-79) = the requested cap when > 0, else the TRUE post-split row count — not simply "the per-task limits". The same layout is in train__fullFT-CoT__qwen2_5_vl.py:165-178 and the gemma4/medgemma/qwen3vl drivers. `grep -rn tmp_prepared_ds --include=*.py` over the repo returns no hits at all; the only other occurrences are two stale launcher comments (script/sft/train__SFT-CoT__Qwen2.5VL7B__*.sh:184).
- **Fix:** Replace the example with the actual default, e.g. `<data_dir>/SFT-CoT_datasets/qwen25vl/ds__AD5500_D110000_TL5500_all121000__resized-wh-512x512` (the `__original` suffix when `--new_shape_hw` is not passed), and say the tokens are the requested caps or, when a cap is unset, the true split sizes.

### H7. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/benchmark-evaluation/references/image-processing-and-token-budgets.md:131`  
*skills:benchmark-evaluation · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "`lmms_eval` auto-injects the few-shot delimiter `"\n\n"` as a stop sequence for `generate_until` tasks. The CoT prompt puts blank lines between `<step-k>` blocks, so on a reasoning model that stop halts generation mid-chain... Passing `--stop_strings '</answer>'` both supplies a clean terminator and signals the wrapper to drop the injected `"\n\n"` stop."
- **Code says:** The vendored engine explicitly removed that behaviour. src/medvision_bm/medvision_lmms_eval/lmms_eval/api/task.py:142-149: "# NOTE: `until` is intentionally NOT defaulted to [fewshot_delimiter]. ... With `until` unset, generation terminates on the model's EOS token; string-level stops are applied only when explicitly requested via --stop_strings." task.py:150-155 sets only `{"do_sample": False}` when generation_kwargs is absent. The wrappers never forward `until` either: src/medvision_bm/medvision_lmms_eval/lmms_eval/models/vllm_qwen25vl.py:287-293 "The task config's `until` ... is NOT forwarded as a decoding stop"; `stop = list(dict.fromkeys(self.stop_strings))`. `grep -rn fewshot_delimiter` over lmms_eval shows it is used only for prompt construction (api/samplers.py:10/32/47, api/task.py:1364/1402). The change landed in commit 2eb7706 "fix: stop using the fewshot delimiter \"\\n\\n\" as a generation stop; rely on EOS, gate string stops on --stop_strings".
- **Fix:** Rewrite the stop-string rationale in all five places that repeat it: image-processing-and-token-budgets.md:131 ("Stop strings" section), launcher-anatomy.md:190-192, troubleshooting.md:52, cli-reference.md:33 ("also drops `lmms_eval`'s auto-injected `\"\\n\\n\"` stop"), and the `stop_string` comments in scripts/model_catalog.json (qwen3vl line 82, gemma4 line 160, glm46v line 241, glm46v-flash line 272, minimax-m3 line 304). Correct statement: no stop sequence is injected; default stopping relies on the model's EOS token, and `--stop_strings '</answer>'` adds an explicit terminator (with `include_stop_str_in_output=True`) for reasoning models that would otherwise run to `max_new_tokens`. Also fix the troubleshooting diagnosis for "a reasoning model stops after the first `<step>`".

### H8. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/benchmark-evaluation/references/model-catalog.md:37`  
*skills:benchmark-evaluation · wrong-name · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "**Extras caveat.** The vendored engine's `pyproject.toml` defines exactly these optional-dependency groups: `audio`, `metrics`, `llava_med`, `qwen2_5_vl`, `medvision_v0`, `qwen3_vl`, `minimax_m3`, `glm4v`, `gemini`, `claude`, `openai`, `kimi`, `reka`, `qwen`, `mmsearch`, `all`. The `lingshu`, `meddr` and `huatuogpt_vision` values that some launchers and drivers pass are **not** defined there — pip prints a \"does not provide the extra\" warning and installs the base package." (also the "(no-op, see below)" annotations on lines 26 and 28)
- **Code says:** All three extras ARE defined in src/medvision_bm/medvision_lmms_eval/pyproject.toml: `meddr` at line 97 (`bitsandbytes==0.45.2`), `lingshu` at line 100 (`transformers==4.52.1`, `qwen-vl-utils==0.0.11`), `huatuogpt_vision` at line 104 (`transformers==4.40.0`). The full group list is audio, metrics, meddr, lingshu, huatuogpt_vision, llava_med, qwen2_5_vl, medvision_v0, qwen3_vl, minimax_m3, glm4v, gemini, claude, openai, kimi, reka, qwen, mmsearch, all (19 groups). The drivers pass them too: src/medvision_bm/benchmark/eval__meddr.py:286 `install_vendored_lmms_eval(proj_dependency="meddr")`, eval__lingshu.py:200, eval__huatuogpt_vision.py:272. The skill's own scripts/model_catalog.json contradicts the caveat (lines 414 and 468 record `"transformers": "4.52.1 (lingshu extra)"` and `"4.40.0 (huatuogpt_vision extra)"`).
- **Fix:** Delete the caveat paragraph (or replace it with the correct 19-group list) and drop the "(no-op)" annotations on the `lingshu` and `huatuogpt_vision` rows. These extras install real transformers pins that materially change the environment.

### H9. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/dataset-and-tasks/references/task-lists.md:54`  
*skills:dataset-and-tasks · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "The SFT/parquet loaders (`load_split_limit_dataset` and `load_split_limit_dataset_tr_val_ts`) take `tag_ds=<family token>` and ... - when `tag_ds == \"BoxSize\"`, rewrite `_BoxCoordinate_ -> _BoxSize_` in the keys, so the `all_tasks__ds_v*` detection `__Train` lists can be used directly." (task-lists.md lines 50-54)
- **Code says:** Only the SFT loader does the rewrite: src/medvision_bm/sft/sft_utils.py:2133-2141 (`if tag_ds == "BoxSize": tasks_dict = {task.replace("_BoxCoordinate_", "_BoxSize_"): n ...}`). The parquet loader src/medvision_bm/dataset/ds_utils.py:2074-equivalent — `load_split_limit_dataset_tr_val_ts` at ds_utils.py:98, reads the keys verbatim at ds_utils.py:181-183 (`tasks_dict = json.load(f); tasks = list(tasks_dict.keys())`) with no rewrite, and builds configs as `task + "_Train"` at ds_utils.py:231. `grep -rn BoxCoordinate src/medvision_bm/dataset/` returns nothing. dataset-info/all_tasks__ds_v1.4.0/tasks_MedVision-detect-CoT__Axial__Train.json keys are e.g. `AbdomenAtlas1.0Mini_BoxCoordinate_Task01_Axial`, so feeding one to build_parquet_ds raises `BuilderConfig 'AbdomenAtlas1.0Mini_BoxCoordinate_Task01_Axial_Train' not found`.
- **Fix:** Split the bullet: state that only `load_split_limit_dataset` (SFT, sft_utils.py:2136) rewrites `_BoxCoordinate_`; `load_split_limit_dataset_tr_val_ts` / `build_parquet_ds` do not, so a dataset-info detection `__Train` list must be rewritten to `_BoxSize_` first (e.g. with scripts/list_tasks.py) before it is passed to `--tasks_list_json_path_detect`.

### H10. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/environment-setup/references/installation.md:9`  
*skills:environment-setup · wrong-version · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "Python `>=3.9` (package metadata). Every launcher, Dockerfile and the PyPI publish workflow use **Python 3.11**; the SFT flash-attn wheel is cp311-only. Use 3.11."
- **Code says:** Three model families deliberately use another Python. script/benchmark-detect/eval__MedDr__detect.sh:9 `conda create -n "${ENV_NAME}" python==3.9 -y`; script/benchmark-detect/eval__LLaVA_Med__detect.sh:9 `python==3.10`; script/benchmark-detect/eval__MiniMax-M3__detect.sh:10 and eval__MiniMax-M3-INT4__*.sh `python==3.12` (12 launchers total across benchmark-detect/TL/AD). Same for the images: dockerfile/Dockerfile.eval_meddr `python=3.9`, Dockerfile.eval_llavamed `python=3.10`, Dockerfile.eval_minimax-m3-int4 `python=3.12`. The pins enforce it: requirements/requirements_eval_meddr.txt pins `flash_attn-2.7.3+...-cp39-cp39-linux_x86_64.whl` and requirements_eval_llavamed.txt pins the `cp310` wheel, so `pip install -r requirements_eval_meddr.txt --no-deps` inside a 3.11 env fails with "not a supported wheel on this platform". (The PyPI workflow part is correct: .github/workflows/publish-pypi.yml:22 `python-version: "3.11"`.)
- **Fix:** Reword to: "The PyPI publish workflow and most launchers/Dockerfiles use Python 3.11, but three model families do not — MedDr 3.9, LLaVA-Med 3.10, MiniMax-M3 / MiniMax-M3-INT4 3.12 (their frozen requirements pin cp39/cp310 flash-attn wheels or a py3.12-only stack). Use 3.11 unless the model is one of those." Add a Python-version column (or a note) to the model table in requirements-catalog.md.

### H11. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/extending-models-and-tasks/references/add-a-task.md:64`  
*skills:extending-models-and-tasks · broken-command · confirmed*

- **Claim:** `tag` | comma-separated labels. Every base config carries `MedVision-<TaskType>` **and** the dataset name, so `--tasks MedVision-TumorLesionSize` or `--tasks BraTS24` runs a whole family.
- **Code says:** The tag is a single YAML scalar, not a list: merging BraTS24_BoxCoordinate_Task01_Axial-CoT.yaml with its base (the same include-merge lmms_eval does) yields tag == 'MedVision-BoxCoordinate,BraTS24' (str). lmms_eval/tasks/__init__.py:451-478 wraps a str tag as `attr_list = [attr_list]` and registers that whole comma-joined string as ONE tag key; nothing splits on ','. lmms_eval/__main__.py:476 does `task_list = args.tasks.split(",")` and lmms_eval/utils.py:234-245 `pattern_match` uses fnmatch against the index keys, so `--tasks MedVision-TumorLesionSize` (and `--tasks BraTS24`) matches nothing and __main__.py:488-491 raises `ValueError: Tasks not found: ...`. The same wrong assumption drives troubleshooting.md:200-212, which blames typos.
- **Fix:** State that the shipped `tag:` value is registered verbatim as a single tag ('MedVision-<TaskType>,<Dataset>'), so a bare family or dataset name does not select anything; a wildcard (`--tasks 'MedVision-TumorLesionSize*'`) or the exact composite string is needed, and MedVision in practice drives tasks one name at a time from tasks_list/*.json. Fix the troubleshooting entry's root cause accordingly.

### H12. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/sft/references/cli-reference.md:215`  
*skills:sft · wrong-version · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "transformers 4.54.0 for qwen25vl and medgemma versus 5.5.0 for gemma4 and qwen3.6vl, with `huggingface_hub` 0.36.0 versus 1.22.0 respectively" — i.e. the qwen25vl frozen SFT requirement set pins huggingface_hub 0.36.0.
- **Code says:** requirements/requirements_sft_qwen25vl.txt pins `huggingface-hub==0.35.3`, not 0.36.0. Only requirements_sft_medgemma.txt pins `huggingface-hub==0.36.0`; requirements_sft_gemma4.txt and requirements_sft_qwen3.6vl.txt pin `huggingface_hub==1.22.0`. (grep over /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/Github/MedVision/requirements/requirements_sft_*.txt)
- **Fix:** Change to: "huggingface_hub 0.35.3 (qwen25vl) / 0.36.0 (medgemma) versus 1.22.0 (gemma4, qwen3.6vl)".

## MEDIUM (81)

### M1. `docsite/source/benchmarking/llm-judge-parsing.md:122`  
*docsite:bench-judge · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** Every visible GPU is used: the work list is split across independent processes (cheap, near-linear speedup) and each process spans the fewest GPUs the model fits in (expensive tensor parallelism, capacity not speed).
- **Code says:** With the shipped defaults `TP=1` (run_llm_parsing.sh:109) and `NUM_SHARDS=1` (run_llm_parsing.sh:124), exactly one GPU is used regardless of how many are visible — the caller must set `TP`/`NUM_SHARDS`/`GPU_NUM`. script/llm-parsing/README.md:301-303 states this explicitly: "By default the run stays on **one GPU** (`TP=1`, one shard) ... Set the knobs to spread over more cards".
- **Fix:** Rewrite as: by default the run stays on one GPU; spreading over more cards is opt-in via `GPU_NUM` (or `TP`/`NUM_SHARDS`), e.g. `GPU_NUM=4 TP=2 bash script/llm-parsing/run_llm_parsing.sh`.

### M2. `docsite/source/benchmarking/llm-judge-parsing.md:80`  
*docsite:bench-judge · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** [`google/gemma-4-31B-it`](https://huggingface.co/google/gemma-4-31B-it) — ~62 GB of bf16 weights, run with 2 GPUs per process.
- **Code says:** The hub id and ~62 GB are correct (judge_config.py:159, 165-170), but `tensor_parallel: 2` (judge_config.py:172) is the registry value only; the driver forces `TP=1` before judge_env.sh reads it (run_llm_parsing.sh:109 + judge_env.sh:66), so a default run puts the whole reader on one GPU. The registry comment at judge_config.py:166-168 notes TP=1 "leaves ~10 GB for the KV cache on an 80 GB card and OOMs on anything smaller".
- **Fix:** Say the registry recommends 2 GPUs per process, but the driver defaults to 1 unless `TP`/`GPU_NUM` is set — and note the KV-cache/OOM consequence on cards below 80 GB.

### M3. `docsite/source/benchmarking/llm-judge-parsing.md:54`  
*docsite:bench-judge · wrong-name · adjusted*

- **Claim:** Re-parsed records land in a sibling `llm-parsed/` folder next to each model's `parsed/`
- **Code says:** The directory is always reader-suffixed: `judge_config.py:438-455` (`llm_parsed_dirname` returns e.g. `"llm-parsed_gemma-4-31b"`, and "the bare `llm-parsed` is never produced"), used by `apply_judge.py:588,599`. script/llm-parsing/README.md:371-373 spells it `llm-parsed_<reader>/`. Line 77 of this page repeats the same bare name ("verify spans and merge into `llm-parsed/`").
- **Fix:** Write `llm-parsed_<reader>/` (e.g. `llm-parsed_gemma-4-31b/`) in both places.
- **Verifier adjustment:** Bare `llm-parsed` appears at lines 54, 77 AND 147; line 147 is a runnable command whose `--parsed_dirname llm-parsed` names a directory the pipeline never creates. Fix all three to `llm-parsed_<reader>` / `llm-parsed_gemma-4-31b`.

### M4. `docsite/source/benchmarking/llm-judge-parsing.md:56`  
*docsite:bench-judge · wrong-name · confirmed*

- **Claim:** Summary reports gain a `__llm-parsed` suffix so they sit beside the published ones instead of overwriting them.
- **Code says:** The report qualifier is `__<parsed_dirname>`: summarize_TL_task.py:59 returns `f"__{parsed_dirname}"`, and the driver passes `parsed_dirname=llm-parsed_gemma-4-31b` (run_llm_parsing.sh:684,725), so the file is `..._summary__llm-parsed_gemma-4-31b`; likewise summarize_judge_task.py:255 writes `summary_judge_task{suffix}__{parsed_dirname}.txt`. script/llm-parsing/README.md:375 says the reports "carry a `__llm-parsed_<reader>` suffix". Line 162 of this page repeats the truncated form ("its `__llm-parsed` twin").
- **Fix:** Use `__llm-parsed_<reader>` (e.g. `__llm-parsed_gemma-4-31b`) at both lines 56 and 162.

### M5. `docsite/source/benchmarking/llm-judge-parsing.md:16`  
*docsite:bench-judge · wrong-number · adjusted*

- **Claim:** Across the 18-model roster the regex rejects a substantial, non-random slice: | Tumour/Lesion | 43,938 | ... | Angle/Distance | 37,080 | ... | Detection | 415,278 |
- **Code says:** The pipeline's rosters now hold 19 models each (`config-TL-CoT.yaml`, `config-AD-CoT.yaml`, `config-detect-CoT.yaml`: 19 `model_display_name` entries apiece), and the Stage 0 count gate is `EXPECTED_ROSTER_COUNTS = {"TL": 46379, "AD": 39140, "Detection": 437349}` (judge_config.py:544, comment at 539-543: "18 paper models + the fullSFT checkpoint"). DESIGN.md:622 states the same explicitly: "roster counts 46,379 / 39,140 / 437,349 (19 models since 2026-08-19; the paper's 18-model totals were 43,938 / 37,080 / 415,278)".
- **Fix:** Either label the table "the paper's 18-model roster (historical)" or update to the current 19-model totals 46,379 / 39,140 / 437,349 (current strict-fail rates 17.4% / 19.9% / 7.7%, from DESIGN.md §13).
- **Verifier adjustment:** category: stale-content, not wrong-number; severity low. The table is internally correct for the 18-model paper roster it names; the defect is that it reads as the pipeline's current state while the shipped rosters are 19 models (46,379 / 39,140 / 437,349). Fix = say "the paper's 18-model roster (historical)" or refresh to the 19-model totals.

The upstream HuatuoChatbot default of 512 truncated CoT responses."), and script/benchmark-AD/eval__HuatuoGPT-Vision-34B__AD.sh sets `max_new_tokens=4096` and passes it. ### M10. `docsite/source/benchmarking/running-evaluations.md:128`  
*docsite:bench-run · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** API-specific flag table: "| `--batch_size` | Concurrent requests. |"
- **Code says:** None of the four API model classes read batch_size at all — `grep -n batch_size` over lmms_eval/models/{openai,claude,gemini,kimi}.py returns nothing — and each `generate_until` iterates requests strictly sequentially in a plain for-loop (openai.py:360-390, claude.py:295-299, gemini.py:452-460). `--batch_size` is only forwarded to the vendored lmms-eval CLI (eval__openai.py:45-46); raising it does not issue concurrent HTTP requests. The entry point's own help even says "Batch size per GPU" (eval__openai.py:113-117).
- **Fix:** Change the description to something like "Forwarded to the lmms-eval harness; API requests are issued sequentially, so this does not add request concurrency (launchers use 1)."

*docsite:bench-run · internal-inconsistency · confirmed*

- **Code says:** Only the 24 TL launchers export both (e.g. script/benchmark-TL/eval__Qwen-2.5-VL__TL.sh:49-50). The 24 AD launchers and 24 detect launchers export only `MedVision_PLANNER_VERSION='1.0.0'` — script/benchmark-AD/eval__Qwen-2.5-VL__AD.sh:48-49, and `grep -rn MedVision_ACK_RELEASE script/benchmark-AD script/benchmark-detect` returns 0 hits. The page's own worked examples are AD runs (`tasks_MedVision-AD-CoT.json`), whose launchers set one export, not two.
- **Fix:** Say that `MedVision_PLANNER_VERSION='1.0.0'` is exported by every launcher and that `MedVision_ACK_RELEASE='1.4.0'` is additionally exported by the T/L launchers only (because release 1.4.0 regenerated only the tumour/lesion annotations).

- **Code says:** All three parsers define `--parsed_dirname` (default "parsed"), `--resps_key` (default "filtered_resps") and `--models` (nargs="+"): src/medvision_bm/benchmark/summarize_AD_task.py:1300-1339, summarize_TL_task.py:1091-1130, summarize_detection_task.py:969-1008. `--resps_key` is load-bearing — its help says records in an llm-parsed*/ directory have the strict key REMOVED so "forgetting this flag aborts rather than silently reporting on nothing" — and the docsite's own benchmarking/llm-judge-parsing.md workflow depends on this pair.

### M16. `docsite/source/reference/cli.md:219`  
*docsite:cli · coverage-gap · adjusted*

- **Claim:** The "Dataset & environment" section documents `python -m medvision_bm.benchmark.env_setup` as the way to "Provision a full evaluation environment", and no other env-setup entry point is mentioned anywhere on the page.
- **Code says:** src/medvision_bm/sft/env_setup.py is a separate CLI (`--data_dir` required, `-r/--requirement` OPTIONAL here, `--lmms_eval_opt_deps`) and is by far the most-invoked entry point in the repo: `grep -rho 'python -m medvision_bm\.[A-Za-z0-9_.-]*' script/ --include=*.sh | sort | uniq -c` gives 93 uses of `medvision_bm.sft.env_setup` (e.g. script/sft/train__SFT-CoT__Qwen2.5VL7B__D110k-AD5.5k-TL5.5k__512x512.sh:133, script/rft/build_parquet_ds__verl__D110k-AD5.5k-TL5.5k__512x512.sh:78) and 0 uses of the documented `medvision_bm.benchmark.env_setup`. A reader following the SFT/RFT sections has no documented env-setup command, and would use the benchmark one where `-r` is required.
- **Fix:** Add an `sft/env_setup` entry under Fine-tuning (or a second sub-heading in Dataset & environment) documenting `python -m medvision_bm.sft.env_setup --data_dir ... [-r requirements.txt] [--lmms_eval_opt_deps ...]`, and note that `-r` is optional there but required in `benchmark.env_setup`.

Severity: low (coverage gap on the reference page only — the command is already shown in docsite/source/fine-tuning/sft.md:40). Fix: add an sft/env_setup row/entry to cli.md noting -r is optional there but required in benchmark.env_setup.

### M25. `docsite/source/extending/add-a-task.md:75`  
*docsite:extending · wrong-name · adjusted*

- **Claim:** "Pick the ones that match the task: detection uses the `avgMAE` / `avgMRE` / `SuccessRate` family, while the metric-geometry tasks use `MAE` / `MRE` (and `NMAE` for size)."
- **Code says:** Every TumorLesionSize base config uses avgMAE/avgMRE, NOT MAE/MRE: src/medvision_bm/medvision_lmms_eval/lmms_eval/tasks/BraTS24/BraTS24_TumorLesionSize_base-CoT.yaml:18 `- metric: avgMAE`, :21 `- metric: avgMRE`, :24 `- metric: SuccessRate`, :27 `- metric: nMAE` (verified identical across all 30 `*_TumorLesionSize_base*.yaml` files in the tasks tree). Only BiometricsFromLandmarks uses MAE/MRE (Ceph-Biometrics-400/Ceph-Biometrics-400_BiometricsFromLandmarks_base-CoT.yaml: `metric: MAE`, `metric: MRE`). Also, `nMAE` is present in BOTH the TL and the AD base configs, so it is not "for size" only; and the metric key is spelled `nMAE` (lowercase n), while `NMAE` is only the aggregator-function suffix (`aggregate_results_NMAE`, medvision_utils.py:3092).
- **Fix:** Rewrite as: detection AND Tumour/Lesion size use the `avgMAE` / `avgMRE` / `SuccessRate` family; Angle/Distance (`BiometricsFromLandmarks`) uses `MAE` / `MRE` / `SuccessRate`; the `nMAE` metric (aggregator `aggregate_results_NMAE`) is added to both TL and AD base configs.
- **Verifier adjustment:** Same claim/severity; evidence count corrected: verified identical across all 36 `*_TumorLesionSize_base*.yaml` files (and all 12 `*_BiometricsFromLandmarks_base*.yaml`, 84 detection base configs). Fix text: detection AND Tumour/Lesion size use avgMAE/avgMRE/SuccessRate; Angle/Distance uses MAE/MRE/SuccessRate; `nMAE` (aggregator `aggregate_results_NMAE`) appears in TL and AD base configs only, not detection.

### M26. `docsite/source/extending/add-a-model.md:106`  
*docsite:extending · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "**Eval driver** — `src/medvision_bm/benchmark/eval__<model>.py` ... It turns the launcher's flags into the `python -m medvision_bm.benchmark.eval__<model>` invocation."
- **Code says:** The direction is inverted. The shell launcher is what issues `python -m medvision_bm.benchmark.eval__<model>` (script/benchmark-detect/eval__Gemini-3.1-Pro__detect.sh:88 `python -m medvision_bm.benchmark.eval__gemini \`). The driver itself turns those flags into a `python3 -m lmms_eval` subprocess: src/medvision_bm/benchmark/eval__gemini.py:36-53 `cmd = ["python3", "-m", "lmms_eval", "--model", lmmseval_module, "--model_args", model_args, "--tasks", task, ...]` (same pattern in eval__qwen3_vl.py:39-55).
- **Fix:** Reword to: "It turns the launcher's flags into the `python3 -m lmms_eval --model <key> --model_args ... --tasks <task>` invocation (one subprocess per task); the launcher is what invokes `python -m medvision_bm.benchmark.eval__<model>`."

### M27. `docsite/source/extending/add-a-task.md:18`  
*docsite:extending · coverage-gap · confirmed*

- **Claim:** The page describes adding a task as "writing a couple of YAML files and, when the behaviour is genuinely new, a few hook functions" and never mentions the task roster JSONs.
- **Code says:** A new task YAML is not run by anything until its task name is added to a roster in `tasks_list/*.json`. The eval drivers take `--tasks_list_json_path` (src/medvision_bm/benchmark/eval__gemini.py:156, :262) and resolve the task names via `load_tasks(tasks_list_json_path)` (eval__gemini.py:287); the launchers point at it (script/benchmark-detect/eval__Gemini-3.1-Pro__detect.sh:54 `tasks_list_json_path="${benchmark_dir}/tasks_list/tasks_MedVision-detect-CoT.json"`, :96). tasks_list/README.md:3 states "This folder defines which MedVision tasks are used for benchmarking and SFT training ... the pipeline reads just the task names (the top-level keys; see `load_tasks` in `src/medvision_bm/utils/utils.py`)."
- **Fix:** Add a step: after creating the task YAML, register its `task:` name in the matching roster under `tasks_list/` (`tasks_MedVision-{detect,TL,AD}-CoT.json`, `..._train_SFT.json`, or `tasks_list/OOD/`), because the eval drivers only run the names listed there via `--tasks_list_json_path`.

### M30. `docsite/source/index.md:85`  
*docsite:getting-started · internal-inconsistency · confirmed*

- **Claim:** Front-page Quickstart block: step 1 is `pip install medvision-bm` (PyPI), and step 3 then runs `--tasks_list_json_path tasks_list/tasks_MedVision-AD-CoT.json --task_status_json_path completed_tasks/AD.json --results_dir Results/MedVision-AD-CoT`.
- **Code says:** `tasks_list/` is a repo-root directory and is not shipped in the wheel: pyproject.toml:36-38 packages only `medvision_bm*` under `src/`, package-data (pyproject.toml:44-49) covers only `medvision_lmms_eval/**`, and MANIFEST.in adds only `src/medvision_bm/medvision_lmms_eval` and `src/medvision_bm/sft/config/*.yaml`. `find src -name tasks_list` returns nothing. The path is opened literally relative to cwd — src/medvision_bm/utils/utils.py:126-138 (`load_tasks` = plain `open(json_file_path)`) — so after a PyPI-only install the command dies with FileNotFoundError. The unit's own installation page says exactly this: docsite/source/getting-started/installation.md:32 — "The PyPI (or GitHub) install gives you the importable package only. If you later decide to benchmark or fine-tune, switch to the full clone so the `script/` and `tasks_list/` directories are present."
- **Fix:** Make step 1 of the front-page Quickstart the full clone (`git clone https://github.com/YongchengYAO/MedVision.git && cd MedVision && pip install .`), or keep `pip install medvision-bm` and add a one-line note that the later steps must be run from a clone because `tasks_list/` is not part of the wheel.

### M31. `docsite/source/releases/v1.1.1.md:3`  
*docsite:releases · internal-inconsistency · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "Release notes for the `medvision_bm` codebase, **v1.1.1**. This is the version this documentation set describes; use the version switcher ... to read the docs for a different release."
- **Code says:** docsite/source/releases/v1.2.0.md:3 carries the identical sentence for v1.2.0, and both pages are in the same toctree (docsite/source/releases/index.md:14-19), so in the `latest`/master build both claim to be the version the docs describe. The build's actual version comes from `medvision_bm.__version__` (docsite/source/conf.py:23-26), and src/medvision_bm/__version__.py:1 is `__version__ = "1.2.0"` — the rest of the docsite is pinned to that release (e.g. docsite/source/getting-started/quickstart.md:17 exports `MedVision_PLANNER_VERSION=1.4.0`, docsite/source/dataset/concepts.md:9 describes v1.4.0 data). The v1.1.1 statement is therefore false in the latest build. The maintainer recipe at docsite/source/releases/index.md:36-38 also never mentions adding this preamble, so it is undocumented drift.
- **Fix:** Drop the "This is the version this documentation set describes" sentence from the per-release pages (keep only "Release notes for the `medvision_bm` codebase, vX.Y.Z"), or emit it only for the release matching `medvision_bm.__version__`; then update the index.md "Adding a version" recipe to state what preamble, if any, a copied note should carry.

### M32. `docsite/source/fine-tuning/sft.md:115`  
*docsite:sft · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** Global caps `--train_sample_limit` and `--val_sample_limit` are always required.
- **Code says:** src/medvision_bm/sft/sft_utils.py:3731-3742 declares both with defaults (`--train_sample_limit` default `-1`, `--val_sample_limit` default `100`) and no `required=True`; `parse_sample_limits` (sft_utils.py:3896-3963) explicitly falls back to those defaults for missing/None values. The launcher itself labels them optional: script/sft/train__fullSFT-CoT__Qwen2.5VL7B__D110k-AD5.5k-TL5.5k__512x512.sh:66 "# [Optional] Sample limits in total" and lines 58-64 "Any limit below may be left unset (commented out); -1 is then passed". The only hard requirement is that at least one `--tasks_list_json_path_*` be given (sft_utils.py:3857-3867).
- **Fix:** Change to: both are optional — unset/`-1` means no total train cap, and `--val_sample_limit` defaults to 100; only `0` is rejected as ambiguous, and at least one `--tasks_list_json_path_*` is required.

### M33. `docsite/source/fine-tuning/sft.md:120`  
*docsite:sft · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** If a limit exceeds the available samples for a task, it is a no-op: the pool is capped at what is available and never oversampled or repeated. (The only with-replacement oversampling is the optional temperature sampler ...)
- **Code says:** True only for the PER-TASK limits (sft_utils.py:2265-2272: `if limit_train_sample > 0 and limit_train_sample < len(dataset['train'])` → select, else no-op). The GLOBAL caps do bootstrap-resample: src/medvision_bm/sft/train__SFT-CoT__qwen2_5_vl.py:229-238 — `if train_limit > train_size: np.random.seed(SEED); indices = np.random.choice(train_size, size=train_limit, replace=True); dataset['train'] = dataset['train'].select(indices)` — and the identical block for `--val_sample_limit` at lines 250-262. Same code in train__fullFT-CoT__qwen2_5_vl.py:203-236.
- **Fix:** Scope the no-op sentence to the per-task limits, and add that `--train_sample_limit` / `--val_sample_limit` larger than the combined pool sample WITH replacement (seeded from `SEED`), i.e. they can duplicate rows.

### M34. `docsite/source/fine-tuning/sft.md:221`  
*docsite:sft · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** The full-parameter driver writes complete model checkpoints directly, so it has no merge/push options (its `--lora_checkpoint_dir` argument is reinterpreted internally as the plain checkpoint directory).
- **Code says:** The `--lora_checkpoint_dir` remap is correct (train__fullFT-CoT__qwen2_5_vl.py:370-371) and there is indeed no merge step, but the full-parameter driver DOES honour a push flag: train__fullFT-CoT__qwen2_5_vl.py:292 passes `push_model=kwargs.get("push_LoRA"), # reuse existing CLI arg` into `prepare_trainer_fullFT`, which sets `push_to_hub=push_model` with `hub_private_repo=True` in its SFTConfig (sft_utils.py:3092-3093). So `--push_LoRA true` on a full-FT run pushes the full model to the Hub.
- **Fix:** Say the full-parameter driver has no MERGE options, but reuses `--push_LoRA` as the push-to-Hub switch for the full model checkpoint (`--merge_model` / `--merged_model_*` / `--merge_only` are ignored).

### M35. `docsite/source/fine-tuning/sft.md:70`  
*docsite:sft · coverage-gap · confirmed*

- **Claim:** Phase 2 code blocks show only `--skip_process_dataset true` (plus FSDP flags) and never pass `--prepared_ds_dir`; the text says phase 2 "loads the cached dataset instead of rebuilding it".
- **Code says:** The shipped launchers tee the prep run to a log and resolve the directory from it before the training launch: script/sft/train__fullSFT-CoT__Qwen2.5VL7B__D110k-AD5.5k-TL5.5k__512x512.sh:~185-200 (`prep_log=.../prepare_dataset.log`, then `prepared_ds_dir="$(sed -n "s/.*Prepared dataset saved at '\([^']*\)'.*/\1/p" "${prep_log}" | tail -n 1)"`, abort if empty) and pass `--prepared_ds_dir ${prepared_ds_dir}` on the accelerate launch. This is load-bearing: in train__SFT-CoT__qwen2_5_vl.py:179-184 the guard is `if prepared_ds_dir is None or not kwargs.get("skip_process_dataset"):` — with `--skip_process_dataset true` but NO `--prepared_ds_dir`, stage 1 (load+split of all tasks) still runs on rank 0 purely to recompute the default directory name (comment at lines 174-179 says exactly this).
- **Fix:** Add `--prepared_ds_dir ${prepared_ds_dir}` to the phase-2 snippets and explain the handoff: the prep run prints "Prepared dataset saved at '<dir>'", the launcher greps that line out of the tee'd log and hands it to training; omitting it makes training re-run the load+split stage.

### M36. `docsite/source/fine-tuning/sft.md:21`  
*docsite:sft · coverage-gap · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "LoRA adapters" / "The LoRA scripts train adapters on a frozen backbone and launch with plain DistributedDataParallel." — the page never states the LoRA path is QLoRA.
- **Code says:** src/medvision_bm/sft/sft_utils.py:2721 docstring: "Build a QLoRA :class:`~trl.SFTTrainer` ... Loads ``base_model_hf`` in 4-bit NF4 quantization"; implementation at sft_utils.py:2799-2805 (`BitsAndBytesConfig(load_in_4bit=True, bnb_4bit_use_double_quant=True, bnb_4bit_quant_type="nf4", ...)`), adapter config at 2825-2836 (`r=16, lora_alpha=32, lora_dropout=0.05, target_modules="all-linear", modules_to_save=["lm_head", "embed_tokens"]`), and `learning_rate = 2e-4` at 2838. None of this (4-bit base, r/alpha, the extra trained `lm_head`/`embed_tokens`, or the 2e-4 LR vs the full-FT 2e-5) appears on the page, and the "Key hyperparameters" table has no learning-rate row at all.
- **Fix:** State that the LoRA recipes are QLoRA (base loaded in 4-bit NF4 with double quant), list r=16 / alpha=32 / dropout=0.05 / all-linear + `modules_to_save=[lm_head, embed_tokens]`, and add a learning-rate row (LoRA 2e-4, full-FT 2e-5 overridable via `MEDVISION_SFT_LR`).

### M37. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/analysis/references/workflows.md:168`  
*skills:analysis · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** Detection x target size **Inputs**: "`medvision_bm` (and `medvision_ds` for the label-level variant, which resolves label names from the benchmark plans)" — i.e. medvision_ds is presented as optional, needed only for the label-level (CSV) variant.
- **Code says:** `medvision_ds` is required by the DEFAULT path the bundled wrapper runs. src/medvision_bm/benchmark/analyze_detection_task_boxsize_vs_random.py:615 calls `get_labelsMap_imgModality_from_seg_benchmark_plan(dataset_name, task_id)` inside `process_jsonl_file_detection_task` (the per-sample metric reader), and again at :484 in `_read_gt_for_random_baseline`. src/medvision_bm/utils/parse_utils.py:597-630 imports `medvision_ds.datasets.<pkg>.preprocess_segmentation` and re-raises ImportError as an uncaught `ValueError`, so without medvision_ds the whole run crashes — not just the label-level variant. The skill's own SKILL.md:30 and scripts/detection_target_size.sh:19 both correctly list medvision_ds as required, so workflows.md contradicts them.
- **Fix:** Change to: "`medvision_bm` **and** `medvision_ds` (both analyzer variants resolve label names from the segmentation benchmark plans); `PyYAML`, `matplotlib`, `pandas` for the figure."

### M38. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/analysis/scripts/detection_target_size.sh:53`  
*skills:analysis · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** `--save-as-png Also write a PNG (default output is PDF).` — and line 135 then prints `done. figure: $out_dir/metrics_boxImgRatio-dotline.pdf (+ .png)`.
- **Code says:** The flag REPLACES the PDF, it does not add to it. detection_target_size.sh:132 appends only `--save_as_png`; src/medvision_bm/benchmark/viz_detection_performance_per_boxImgRatio.py:312-314 computes `formats = [f for f, on in (("png", args.save_as_png), ("pdf", args.save_as_pdf)) if on] or ["pdf"]`, so `--save_as_png` alone yields `['png']` (verified by evaluating that expression: True/False -> ['png']). Only `--save_as_pdf` as well yields both. Consequently, with `--save-as-png` the completion line at :135 names a `.pdf` that was never written. Dry run confirms the emitted command is `... --save_as_png` with no `--save_as_pdf`.
- **Fix:** Either append `--save_as_pdf` alongside `--save_as_png` when `png=1` (so both formats are written, matching the documented text), or reword the help to "write a PNG **instead of** the PDF" and fix the final echo. The same wording is duplicated verbatim in references/cli-reference.md:360 and must be updated with it; scripts/detection_target_size.sh:13 ("PNG with --save-as-png") is already correct.

### M39. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/analysis/references/troubleshooting.md:46`  
*skills:analysis · internal-inconsistency · confirmed*

- **Claim:** Row "The A/D task produced a full set of `_filtered` files identical to the unfiltered ones" -> fix: "do not pass the removed-samples flags to A/D; the wrapper never does, and **the A/D analyzers do not accept them**".
- **Code says:** The analyzer that produces the described symptom — `summarize_CDA_task.py` — does accept `--removed_samples_dir` / `--removed_samples_filename` on an A/D task directory, with no task-type gate: scripts/cda/summarize_CDA_task.py:662,673 register the flags, and `_metrics_filename` (:136-147) / `_print_and_save_cross_model_report` (:400) append `_filtered` whenever `removed_samples_dir` is truthy, independent of file kind. scripts/cda/cda_stats.py:94-100 states this explicitly: "That is what makes passing the flag to an A/D run harmless: those datasets ship no such file." The repo runner records the observed effect (script/analyze/clinical-decision-analysis/run_CDA_analysis.sh:29-31: "Passing the flag to the A/D run removed nothing yet still wrote a full set of '_filtered' files byte-identical to the unfiltered ones (38 of them)"). The row is self-contradictory: if the analyzer rejected the flags, the symptom could not occur. (Only `analyze_process_accuracy_AD.py` / `analyze_equation_accuracy_AD.py` genuinely lack the flags.)
- **Fix:** Reword to: "the CDA analyzer accepts the flags for any task dir and stamps `_filtered` on the names, but A/D datasets ship no removed-samples JSON so nothing is excluded — the wrapper therefore never passes them to the A/D run; the process/equation A/D analyzers do not have the flags at all." Also soften cda.md:214 ("A/D artifacts never carry `_filtered`") to "never carry `_filtered` when run through `run_cda.sh`".

- **Code says:** Two of the six batch for real. src/medvision_bm/medvision_lmms_eval/lmms_eval/models/medgemma.py:287-297 iterates `for i in range(0, len(pending), self.batch_size)` and calls `self.infer(questions=batch_contexts, ...)`, which on the default pipeline path (medgemma.py:210-212, `use_pipeline: bool = True` at line 38, and eval__medgemma.py:221-224 passes `use_pipeline=True`) runs `self.pipe(text=batch_messages, max_new_tokens=..., batch_size=self.batch_size)`. src/medvision_bm/medvision_lmms_eval/lmms_eval/models/lingshu.py:280-290 does the same and lingshu.py:170-200 builds one padded `self._processor(text=batch_texts, ..., padding=True)` and a single `self._model.generate(...)` per batch. Only meddr.py:133, huatuogpt_vision.py:220, llava_med.py:145 and healthgpt.py:229 iterate one request at a time.
- **Fix:** Say `batch_size` is cosmetic for MedDr, HuatuoGPT-Vision, LLaVA-Med and HealthGPT only; MedGemma (pipeline path) and Lingshu (padded batch) do batch, so raising `batch_size_per_gpu` there both raises throughput and raises VRAM. These surface in `--dry-run` output as `pins`.

The gemma4 module itself installs a floor, not a pin: src/medvision_bm/benchmark/eval__gemma4.py:23 `def install_transformers_for_gemma4(transformers_version="5.5.0")` → `pip install "transformers>=5.5.0"`.
- **Fix:** Regenerate the `pins` blocks from requirements/requirements_eval_*.txt, and label whether each value is the requirements pin or the module's `ensure_hf_hub_installed` / built-in install value — they differ for gemma4, glm4v, qwen3vl, medgemma and minimax-m3-int4.

Fix = label the source (or emit both), not renumber. Note gemma4's transformers "5.5.0" is a >= floor, not a pin.

### M51. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/biomedparse-ablation/references/cli-reference.md:40`  
*skills:biomedparse-ablation · other · confirmed*

- **Claim:** NPZ format table: "| `imgs` | uint8 array `(H, W)` (2-D slice, normalized to 0-255) |"
- **Code says:** The writer stores a 3-D array, not a 2-D one. script/ablation/biomedparse/src/prepare_test_data_detect.py:61 `img_3d = np.expand_dims(img_2d_normalized, axis=0)` -> :62 `return [img_3d]`; :100-101 `pil_img = visuals[0]; img_array = np.array(pil_img)`; :116-123 `np.savez(save_path, imgs=img_array, ...)`. Identical in prepare_test_data_tl.py:33-34, 69-70, 85-92. Confirmed on a real file: `np.load('data/test_npz/tl/BraTS24__BraTS-GLI-02061-103-t2f__dim2__idx149__lbl4.npz')['imgs'].shape` -> `(1, 182, 218)`, dtype uint8. Both evaluators therefore squeeze it: eval_detect.py:395-398 and eval_tl.py:829-833 do `np.squeeze(img_rgb)` before use.
- **Fix:** Change the row to: `imgs` | uint8 array `(1, H, W)` — the 2-D slice with a leading singleton axis (`np.expand_dims(..., axis=0)`); consumers call `np.squeeze` first (as `eval_detect.py` / `eval_tl.py` do).

- **Code says:** The dataset-info detection `__Train` catalogues use eval-namespace family tokens: dataset-info/all_tasks__ds_v1.4.0/tasks_MedVision-detect-CoT__Axial__Train.json first key is `AbdomenAtlas1.0Mini_BoxCoordinate_Task01_Axial` (no `-CoT`, so the script's grep guard stays silent). src/medvision_bm/dataset/ds_utils.py:231 appends `_Train` verbatim with no `BoxCoordinate->BoxSize` rewrite, and no such HF config exists (dataset-info/dataset-configs/v1.4.0/ConfigurationsList_All.csv contains only `_BoxSize_` names). The documented invocation therefore fails for detection.
- **Fix:** Restrict the recommendation to `tasks_MedVision-detect__train_SFT.json` (which already uses `_BoxSize_`) for the detection family, or extend the pre-flight guard at lines 72-76 to also reject/rewrite `_BoxCoordinate_` keys when `--tasks-json-detect` is used.

Biometry — BraTS24 v1.4.0 52.61 MB, MSD v1.4.0 22.18 MB, autoPET-III v1.4.0 14.40 MB, MAMA-MIA v1.4.0 10.99 MB; 16 of 41 biometry plans exceed 0.5 MB. ### M63. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/extending-models-and-tasks/SKILL.md:110`  
*skills:extending-models-and-tasks · coverage-gap · adjusted*

- **Claim:** 'References and scripts' plus the 11-row 'Checklist' in add-a-model.md present the complete set of files to read and to touch when adding a model or task.
- **Code says:** The repository ships its own maintainer guides for exactly this unit's topic — docs/New-Models-Guide.md (311 lines: 'Adding New Models to MedVision', embeds the full AVAILABLE_MODELS dict at lines 22-73 and the whole get_resized_img_shape dispatch at lines 87-160, plus an 'API Models' section at line 246), docs/New-Tasks-Guide.md (127 lines), docs/Model-Image-Processing.md (286 lines) and docs/model-token-budget.md (90 lines). `grep -rn 'docs/'` over the whole unit and `grep -rn 'New-Models-Guide|New-Tasks-Guide|Model-Image-Processing'` over skills/medvision/ return nothing, so neither the references list nor the checklist mentions them; a model added by following the checklist leaves docs/New-Models-Guide.md's embedded registry and dispatch stale.

### M65. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/llm-judge-parsing/SKILL.md:94`  
*skills:llm-judge-parsing · wrong-version · adjusted*

- **Claim:** "The judge needs a **newer vLLM than the evaluation code pins** (registry: `vllm==0.19.0` with a Transformers 5.x line), so the evaluation env is always the wrong interpreter." (SKILL.md lines 92-94, Load-bearing facts)
- **Code says:** requirements/requirements_eval_gemma4.txt pins the SAME stack as the judge registry, not an older one: line 295 `vllm==0.19.0`, line 282 `transformers==5.10.2`, line 276 `torch==2.10.0`, line 157 `nvidia-nccl-cu12==2.28.7`. Compare script/llm-parsing/requirements-gemma-4-31b.txt:28 `vllm==0.19.0`, requirements-gemma-4-31b-post.txt `transformers==5.10.2` + `nvidia-nccl-cu12==2.28.7`, judge_config.py:184 `torch_pin: torch==2.10.0`. requirements/requirements_eval_glm4v.txt:299 pins `vllm==0.19.1`, i.e. NEWER than the judge. The repo says so itself: script/llm-parsing/requirements-gemma-4-31b-post.txt (comment block) — "the validated eval stack (requirements/requirements_eval_gemma4.txt) ships the same combination and runs: it carries compressed-tensors==0.14.0.1 and nvidia-nccl-cu12==2.28.7 against transformers 5.10.2, exactly as here." Only 7 of the 10 eval requirement files that pin vllm are on 0.10.x/0.11.0 (grep '^vllm==' requirements/*.txt).
- **Fix:** Replace "newer vLLM than the evaluation code pins ... always the wrong interpreter" with the accurate statement: most per-model eval envs pin vLLM 0.10.x-0.14.0 and cannot host the judge, while requirements_eval_gemma4.txt happens to pin the identical stack (vllm 0.19.0 / transformers 5.10.2 / torch 2.10.0). Keep the operational rule (separate venvs; never cross-install) but drop "always" and "newer than". The same wording appears at references/troubleshooting.md:24 and references/judge-environment.md:3.
- **Verifier adjustment:** SKILL.md:92-94 overstates the version relationship: most per-model eval envs pin vLLM 0.10.x-0.14.0 and cannot host the judge, but requirements_eval_gemma4.txt pins the identical stack (vllm 0.19.0 / transformers 5.10.2 / torch 2.10.0) and requirements_eval_glm4v.txt pins vllm 0.19.1 (newer). Drop "newer than" and "always"; keep the separate-venv rule. Severity: low (rationale wording, not an actionable-instruction error). Same wording at troubleshooting.md:24.

### M71. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/results-parsing-and-metrics/scripts/parse_and_summarize.sh:128`  
*skills:results-parsing-and-metrics · broken-command · confirmed*

- **Claim:** The wrapper accepts `--model-dir` together with `--resps-key` for any task type and appends `--resps_key "$RESPS_KEY"` to the summarize command (line 128); the usage block (line 53) documents `--resps-key KEY record key with the prediction (default filtered_resps; LLM_filtered_resps for llm-parsed*/)` with no task-type/mode restriction, and workflows.md:94 says model-dir mode simply 'writes only the per-model JSON files'.
- **Code says:** src/medvision_bm/benchmark/summarize_detection_task.py:860-878 — `_process_single_model_directory(model_dir, limit, processes=None, parsed_dirname="parsed", resps_key="filtered_resps")` calls `process_parsed_file_in_model_folder(model_dir, limit, processes=processes, parsed_dirname=parsed_dirname)` and DROPS `resps_key`, so it stays "filtered_resps". `assert_resps_key` (src/medvision_bm/utils/parse_utils.py:103-117) then raises SystemExit `[FATAL] ... has no 'filtered_resps' key` on an llm-parsed*/ folder. The TL and AD summarizers do forward it (src/medvision_bm/benchmark/summarize_TL_task.py:990-998; summarize_AD_task.py:1179+). So `parse_and_summarize.sh --task-type Detection --model-dir <dir> --parsed-dirname llm-parsed_<judge> --resps-key LLM_filtered_resps` aborts as written.
- **Fix:** State in cli-reference.md/workflows.md (and warn in the wrapper) that `--resps_key` is honored by summarize_detection_task only in `--task_dir` mode — the `--model_dir` path ignores it (upstream bug at summarize_detection_task.py:876-878); use `--task_dir ... --models <name>` for a single detection model on llm-parsed records.

### M72. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/results-parsing-and-metrics/references/metrics.md:75`  
*skills:results-parsing-and-metrics · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "`parse_outputs`' own `<ts>_results.json` uses the same rules: avgMAE,none/avgMRE,none over successes, avgIoU,none over finite values (failures = 0), SuccessRate,none and MRE<k over the total count." — combined with the unqualified rule stated at SKILL.md:58 and metrics.md:61 that "`MRE<1.0` equals `SuccessRate` because the last bucket is `[0.9, inf)`".
- **Code says:** The bucket construction differs between the two writers. summarize_TL_task.py:226 (and summarize_AD_task.py:_update_mre_counters) use `threshold_index = min(int(value * 10), 9)`, so the last bucket is [0.9, inf) and MRE<1.0 == SuccessRate. parse_outputs.py:117-138 `_update_re_counts` is an if/elif chain ending at `elif re_val < 1.0: count_RE_ls[9] += 1` with NO else, so every MRE >= 1.0 lands in no bucket and `MRE<1.0` = count(MRE<1.0)/total != SuccessRate. Real file Results/MedVision-TL-v2-CoT/GLM-4.6V-Flash/parsed/20260621_062934_results.json: "SuccessRate,none": 0.8966 but "MRE<1.0": 0.4253 (while the group rows of parsed/summary_metrics_TL_Task_filtered.json have SuccessRate == MRE<1.0 exactly).
- **Fix:** Scope the identity: say `MRE<1.0 == SuccessRate` holds only in `summary_metrics_*` (summarizer buckets clamp to index 9); in `parse_outputs`' `<ts>_results.json` samples with MRE >= 1.0 are counted in no bucket, so MRE<1.0 there is strictly the fraction with MRE < 1.0 and is normally far below SuccessRate.

### M76. `skills/medvision/references/troubleshooting.md:62`  
*skills:root · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "Eval says a task is done and skips it | `completed_tasks/completed_tasks_<task_tag>.json` marks it complete | pass `--skip_update_status` for debugging runs or remove the entry"
- **Code says:** `--skip_update_status` only suppresses the *write*, never the skip: src/medvision_bm/benchmark/eval__gemma3.py:249-251 runs `completed_tasks = load_tasks_status(...)` / `if task in completed_tasks: print("Task {task} already completed. Skipping...")` unconditionally, while :299-300 is `if not args.skip_update_status: update_task_status(...)`. The same unconditional-check / conditional-write pair appears in eval__qwen2_5_vl.py:331,383, eval__claude.py:253,303, eval__medgemma.py:215,256 and eval__medvision-model-rft.py:341,398. Its own help string is "Skip updating task status after completion -- useful for debugging."

### M79. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/sft/references/data-preparation.md:237`  
*skills:sft · wrong-name · confirmed*

- **Code says:** `labels` never exists on disk. src/medvision_bm/sft/sft_utils.py:2416 lists it in `keys_to_keep`, but `clean_dataset` (sft_utils.py:2343-2367) only calls `remove_columns` — it cannot create a column — and no `_format_data_*` mapping function ever assigns `example["labels"]` (only `messages`, `processed_images`, `image_file_png`). Every real prepared dataset on disk confirms it: all 9 dirs under Data/SFT-CoT_datasets/*/*/train/dataset_info.json have features `['image_file','slice_dim','slice_idx','messages','image_file_png','__task_name']`. `labels` is produced by the collator at batch time (SFTConfig `label_names=["labels"]`, sft_utils.py:2877).

### M80. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/sft/references/cli-reference.md:118`  
*skills:sft · stale-behavior · adjusted*

- **Claim:** "`--merge_only` … Skip training entirely; merge the last checkpoint and optionally push." (repeated in references/workflows.md:218 "skips training entirely and merges the last checkpoint", and SKILL.md:84)
- **Code says:** It merges the adapter located directly at `--lora_checkpoint_dir`, not the newest `checkpoint-*` subdirectory. train__SFT-CoT__qwen2_5_vl.py:363-371 calls `merge_models(base_model_hf=..., lora_checkpoint_dir=lora_checkpoint_dir, ...)`; sft_utils.py:3339 does `PeftModel.from_pretrained(model, lora_checkpoint_dir)`. `get_last_checkpoint` is used only on the resume path (train__SFT-CoT__qwen2_5_vl.py:334). A run interrupted before `trainer.save_model()` leaves only `checkpoint-N/` subdirs and `--merge_only true` will fail to load an adapter.
- **Fix:** Say "merges the adapter saved at `--lora_checkpoint_dir` itself (written by `trainer.save_model()` at the end of training) — not the newest `checkpoint-*` subdirectory".
- **Verifier adjustment:** cli-reference.md:118 and workflows.md:218 phrase --merge_only as merging "the last checkpoint"; it actually merges the adapter saved directly at --lora_checkpoint_dir (written by trainer.save_model()), never the newest checkpoint-* subdirectory (get_last_checkpoint is used only on the resume path, train__SFT-CoT__qwen2_5_vl.py:334). SKILL.md:84 does NOT contain this wording. Severity: low (ambiguous wording; only misleads for a run interrupted before save_model()).

The catalogue's own table row at line 58 contradicts the preamble by saying that recipe has "no MEDVISION_SFT_* knobs".

*docsite:api · wrong-version · confirmed*

- **Claim:** except Exception: # pragma: no cover - version is best-effort\n version = release = "1.1.1"
- **Code says:** src/medvision_bm/__version__.py:1 is `__version__ = "1.2.0"`; `git tag` lists v1.2.0 as the newest tag and docsite/source/releases/v1.2.0.md exists. `git log -p -- docsite/source/conf.py` shows the fallback was last bumped in the v1.1.1 release commit (ddfdfe4) and was not touched for v1.2.0, so the hard-coded fallback is one release behind. It only bites if the `from medvision_bm import __version__` import at conf.py:23 fails (which it should not, since medvision_bm/__init__.py has no heavy imports), but the site would then silently advertise 1.1.1.
- **Fix:** Bump the fallback to "1.2.0" (or drop the try/except and let the build fail loudly if the version cannot be imported) and add conf.py to the release checklist in docs/codebase-release/.

### L2. `docsite/source/conf.py:41`  
*docsite:api · wrong-path · confirmed*

- **Claim:** templates_path = ["_templates"]
- **Code says:** `ls -d docsite/source/_templates` -> "No such file or directory"; the only directories under docsite/source are _static, benchmarking, dataset, extending, fine-tuning, getting-started, reference, releases. The referenced template directory does not exist, so the setting is dead config (and, together with `autosummary_generate = True` at conf.py:56, misleading: no `.. autosummary::` directive exists anywhere under docsite/source, verified by grep).
- **Fix:** Either create docsite/source/_templates (with the autosummary templates the conf.py docstring implies) or delete the `templates_path` line and the unused `autosummary_generate` setting.

### L3. `docsite/source/reference/api/verl_utils.md:3`  
*docsite:api · internal-inconsistency · confirmed*

- **Claim:** Build verl-format parquet datasets for reinforcement fine-tuning, including the per-task formatting functions and an optional held-out test split.
- **Code says:** All six per-task formatters in src/medvision_bm/rft/verl/verl_utils.py are private: `_format_data_TumorLesionTask_CoT_verl` (line 37), `_format_data_TumorLesionTask_verl` (134), `_format_data_AngleDistanceTask_CoT_verl` (209), `_format_data_AngleDistanceTask_verl` (335), `_format_data_DetectionTask_CoT_verl` (420), `_format_data_DetectionTask_verl` (510). The page uses `.. automodule:: ... :members:` (verl_utils.md:6-9) and conf.py:60-64 sets only members/undoc-members/show-inheritance -- no `:private-members:` anywhere in docsite (grep for "private-members" returns nothing). The rendered page therefore contains only `prepare_dataset_for_verl` (599) and `prepare_dataset_for_verl_with_testset` (703), not the formatting functions the prose promises.
- **Fix:** Either add `:private-members:` / an explicit `:members:` list for the `_format_data_*_verl` helpers, or reword the intro to say the module wraps the per-task formatters internally and exposes only the two `prepare_dataset_for_verl*` entry points.

- **Code says:** The table omits `TASK_DIR_<task>` / `ROSTER_YAML_<task>`, which are the documented mechanism for pointing the pipeline at a different Results tree and roster — run_llm_parsing.sh:56-60 ("How the OOD splits reused this pipeline without a second copy of it") and script/llm-parsing/README.md:249-250 plus its "Recipe: judge other Results trees (the OOD splits)" section (README.md:195). It also omits `GPU_NUM`, the one-knob GPU spelling (run_llm_parsing.sh:73-77), which matters given the single-GPU defaults.

(a) Wrong defaults (medium, correctness): `TP` is documented as 'default per reader; 2 for gemma-4-31b' and `NUM_SHARDS` as 'default: visible GPUs / TP', and the following paragraph claims 'Every visible GPU is used' — but run_llm_parsing.sh:109 sets TP default 1 (explicitly overriding the reader registry) and line 124 sets NUM_SHARDS default 1, so an unconfigured run stays on ONE GPU (README.md:252-254 states this correctly). (b) Coverage gap (low): the table omits `GPU_NUM` (run_llm_parsing.sh:72-77, 115-123) and `TASK_DIR_<task>` / `ROSTER_YAML_<task>` (run_llm_parsing.sh:56-60, 353-376; README.md 'Recipe: judge other Results trees (the OOD splits)' at README.md:195), the documented way the OOD splits are judged; neither string appears anywhere in docsite/source/. Fix: correct the TP/NUM_SHARDS rows and the 'every visible GPU' sentence to the single-GPU default, and add rows for GPU_NUM and TASK_DIR_<task>/ROSTER_YAML_<task>.

### L7. `docsite/source/benchmarking/llm-judge-parsing.md:169`  
*docsite:bench-judge · coverage-gap · confirmed*

- **Claim:** Judge output is **not reproducible** run to run, even with greedy decoding on identical hardware — the cause is numerical non-determinism in the inference stack, not sampling.
- **Code says:** True of the pipeline as shipped, but the referenced report now records that the fix exists in the pinned engine: docs/LLM-Judge-Reproducibility.md:238 and §7.1 (lines 240-256) — "batch-invariant kernels (`VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT=1`) | the only real fix | ... **available in the pinned `vllm==0.19.0`**", verified 2026-08-20 against `.cache/judge-env_gemma-4-31b`, with the caveats that it constrains the attention backend and forces single-channel NCCL. No pipeline file sets or exposes it (grep for `VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT` over script/llm-parsing/*.py and *.sh returns nothing).
- **Fix:** Add one clause: the pinned vllm 0.19.0 does ship a batch-invariance switch (`VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT=1`) that the pipeline deliberately does not enable, at a throughput cost — so "release the judge-out file" remains the policy.

The launcher confirms it (script/benchmark-detect/eval__Gemini-3.1-Pro__detect.sh:91 `--google_model_code $google_model_code`). `--model_hf_id` exists only on the 17 local/vLLM drivers (e.g. eval__qwen3_vl.py:129).
- **Fix:** Annotate the diagram: local/vLLM drivers take `--model_name --model_hf_id`; API drivers take `--model_name --<provider>_model_code` (`--anthropic_model_code`, `--google_model_code`, `--openai_model_code`, `--kimi_model_code`), which the driver forwards as `model_hf=` inside `--model_args`.

### L32. `docsite/source/releases/v1.2.0.md:11`  
*docsite:releases · wrong-number · adjusted*

- **Claim:** "Every launch script now states its output length instead of relying on a default: 4096 for the 48 open-weight scripts, raised to 16000 for GLM-4.6V, MedGemma-27B and Llama-3.2-Vision, which were running out of room (83e3187)"
- **Code says:** There are 57 open-weight (non-API) launchers, not 48, and only 46 of them use 4096. `grep -rh '^max_new_tokens=' script/benchmark-{detect,TL,AD}` returns exactly: 46x `max_new_tokens=4096`, 5x `max_new_tokens=16000`, 6x `max_new_tokens=16384`. The 5 at 16000 are script/benchmark-detect/eval__Llama-3.2-Vision__detect.sh:?, script/benchmark-AD/eval__GLM-4.6V__AD.sh, script/benchmark-AD/eval__GLM-4.6V-Flash__AD.sh, script/benchmark-TL/eval__MedGemma-27B__TL.sh, script/benchmark-AD/eval__MedGemma-27B__AD.sh. The 6 at 16384 are the MiniMax-M3 / MiniMax-M3-INT4 launchers (script/benchmark-{detect,TL,AD}/eval__MiniMax-M3{,-INT4}__*.sh), a third budget the sentence never mentions. The 15 API launchers use `max_tokens=` instead (9x 16000, 6x 4096), so 72 total = 57 + 15. `git grep -h '^max_new_tokens=' 83e3187 -- script/benchmark-*` gives the same 46/5/6 split, so the count was already wrong at the commit it cites.
- **Fix:** Rewrite as: "4096 for 46 of the 57 open-weight scripts, raised to 16000 for GLM-4.6V, GLM-4.6V-Flash, MedGemma-27B and Llama-3.2-Vision, and 16384 for MiniMax-M3."
- **Verifier adjustment:** docsite/source/releases/v1.2.0.md:11 — the '48' comes from commit 83e3187's own wording (the 48 launchers it newly gave a budget); 9 open-weight launchers already had one, so there are 57 open-weight scripts in all: 46 at 4096, 5 at 16000 (GLM-4.6V, GLM-4.6V-Flash, MedGemma-27B x2, Llama-3.2-Vision) and 6 at 16384 (MiniMax-M3 / -INT4), a third budget the sentence never mentions. Rewrite as: '4096 for 46 of the 57 open-weight scripts, raised to 16000 for GLM-4.6V, GLM-4.6V-Flash, MedGemma-27B and Llama-3.2-Vision, and 16384 for MiniMax-M3.'

Their argparse surfaces are not identical: build_parquet_ds__checkpointed.py:273 adds `--shard_size` (absent from build_parquet_ds.py:19-171); build_parquet_ds_with_testset.py adds `--test_sample_limit`, `--test_sample_limit_per_subset`, `--test_sample_limit_task_AD/_Detection/_TL` and `--train_sample_limit_per_subset`; build_parquet_ds_with_testset__checkpointed.py adds both sets.

The '(and its __checkpointed twin)' grouping into three bullets is intentional and not itself an inconsistency. Fix: 'Four modules share a common CLI core (checkpointed variants add --shard_size; test-set variants add --test_sample_limit* / --*_per_subset); pick by dataset size and intent:'. Severity low.

### L35. `docsite/source/fine-tuning/rft.md:50`  
*docsite:rft · coverage-gap · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "`medvision_bm.rft.verl.build_parquet_ds__checkpointed` — writes intermediate shards so a run can survive an out-of-memory kill and resume. Use it for the very large detection splits (the ~1M-sample script above relies on it)." The Key-flags table (lines 55-64) lists no shard-related flag.
- **Code says:** src/medvision_bm/rft/verl/build_parquet_ds__checkpointed.py:271-280 defines `--shard_size` (default 50000, the one knob that controls peak RAM per shard; docstring lines 5, 16-20 spell out the memory budget). It is the only flag unique to the recommended large-scale builder, and it appears nowhere in the docsite (grep for "shard_size" over docsite/source/ returns no hits).
- **Fix:** Add a `--shard_size` row to the Key flags table (or a sentence in the Builder-variants bullet): "`--shard_size` (checkpointed builders only, default 50000) — training rows per shard; the main lever on peak RAM."

### L36. `docsite/source/fine-tuning/rft.md:62`  
*docsite:rft · coverage-gap · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "`--train_sample_limit` / `--val_sample_limit` | Global caps applied after the tasks are concatenated. Keep these equal to the sum of the per-task limits so nothing is silently truncated."
- **Code says:** src/medvision_bm/rft/verl/build_parquet_ds.py:300-332 — when the global limit EXCEEDS the concatenated size the builder does not truncate but bootstraps: `np.random.seed(SEED); indices = np.random.choice(train_size, size=train_limit, replace=True); dataset["train"].select(indices)` (same for validation). The launchers themselves flag this (script/rft/build_parquet_ds__verl__D110k-AD5.5k-TL5.5k__512x512.sh:39 "NOTE: Allow sampling with replacement if limit exceeds dataset size"), but the docsite never mentions it, so a user who over-sets the cap silently gets duplicated rows rather than an error or a truncation.
- **Fix:** Extend the row: "...Keep these equal to the sum of the per-task limits: a smaller value silently truncates, and a larger one silently oversamples with replacement (seeded bootstrap)."

The first half of the claim (no YAML sets max_new_tokens) is correct.

### L49. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/benchmark-evaluation/references/hardware-and-parallelism.md:57`  
*skills:benchmark-evaluation · coverage-gap · adjusted*

- **Claim:** "`eval__medgemma`, `eval__lingshu`, `eval__meddr`, `eval__huatuogpt_vision`, `eval__llava_med` and `eval__healthgpt` wrap `lmms_eval` in `python -m accelerate.commands.launch --num_processes=<GPUs>`" (repeated at cli-reference.md:87, workflows.md:68, model-catalog.md:88)
- **Code says:** The actual command is `python3 -m accelerate.commands.launch --num_processes=<N> --main_process_port=29501` — src/medvision_bm/benchmark/eval__medgemma.py:33-37, eval__lingshu.py:34-38, eval__meddr.py:114-118, eval__huatuogpt_vision.py:67-71, eval__healthgpt.py:148-152; eval__llava_med.py:86-90 uses `--main_process_port=29502`. The hard-coded rendezvous port means two concurrent HF data-parallel runs on the same node collide ("address already in use"), which none of the four pages or troubleshooting.md mentions.
- **Fix:** Add `--main_process_port=29501` (29502 for LLaVA-Med) to the reconstructed command, and add a troubleshooting row for the port collision when two HF runs share a node.
- **Verifier adjustment:** Downgrade from an inconsistency to a documentation gap: the pages paraphrase the wrapper and omit the hard-coded `--main_process_port` (29501 everywhere, 29502 for eval__llava_med), so two concurrent HF data-parallel runs on one node collide; the launch binary is also `python3`. Suggested fix (mention the port + add a troubleshooting row) stands.

Running it now yields sha256:be4538f9…, excluding __pycache__ yields sha256:1787aff6…, and disco/routing_decision/classification.json records sha256:40d4651a… — i.e. the recorded digest is already unreproducible. ### L83. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/results-parsing-and-metrics/references/metrics.md:48`  
*skills:results-parsing-and-metrics · stale-behavior · adjusted*

- **Claim:** "`F1 = 2I / (A_pred + A_gt)` (Dice; NaN if both areas are 0), `Precision = I / A_pred` (NaN if `A_pred = 0`), `Recall = I / A_gt` (`ValueError` if `A_gt <= 0`, caught by `cal_metrics_detection_task` and turned into a failure)" — repeated in api-reference.md:23-25 and relied on by metrics.md:63 ("N (minus rare NaN from zero-area boxes)").
- **Code says:** Those NaN/ValueError branches are unreachable: src/medvision_bm/utils/parse_utils.py:342-343 (cal_F1), :405-406 (cal_Precision) and :461-462 (cal_Recall) return 0.0 as soon as `ix1 >= ix2 or iy1 >= iy2`, and a zero-area box always satisfies that test before the `denominator > 0` / `pred_area > 0` / `target_area <= 0` checks at :354-355, :415 and :470-471. Executed the extracted functions: cal_F1([.2,.2,.2,.2],[.2,.2,.2,.2]) -> 0.0, cal_Precision(zero-area pred) -> 0.0, cal_Recall(_, zero-area target) -> 0.0 (no exception).
- **Fix:** Say that degenerate (zero-area) boxes fall out of the no-overlap test and score 0.0 for F1/Precision/Recall — the NaN and ValueError branches in the source are dead code — and drop the "minus rare NaN from zero-area boxes" caveat in the metrics.md:63 denominator column.
- **Verifier adjustment:** metrics.md:48 / api-reference.md:23-25: zero-area boxes are caught by the no-overlap test and score 0.0 for F1/Precision/Recall, not NaN; drop the 'minus rare NaN from zero-area boxes' caveat at metrics.md:63. The NaN results only arise from NaN-valued coordinate predictions, and cal_Recall's documented ValueError branch (parse_utils.py:470-471) is unreachable — nothing raises, so the 'caught by cal_metrics_detection_task and turned into a failure' clause never fires.

### L84. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/results-parsing-and-metrics/references/workflows.md:97`  
*skills:results-parsing-and-metrics · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** Under "Flags shared by the three summarizers": "`--skip_model_wo_parsed_files` (task_dir mode only): otherwise the report step raises `FileNotFoundError` on the first model folder without the metrics JSON."
- **Code says:** True only for TL and AD, which open the metrics file unguarded (summarize_TL_task.py:694 `with open(metrics_file, "r")`; summarize_AD_task.py:776 same). summarize_detection_task.py:714-716 guards it: `grouped_metrics_path = ...; if os.path.exists(grouped_metrics_path):` — a model folder without the metrics JSON is silently omitted from `summary_detection_task.txt` / `summary_metrics_all_models_detect_Task.json` and the run exits 0 (this silent-skip behaviour is exactly what the assert_resps_key docstring at parse_utils.py:71-73 describes).
- **Fix:** Qualify: TL/AD raise FileNotFoundError, while summarize_detection_task silently skips such folders and writes a report missing those models — which is why --models/--skip_model_wo_parsed_files matter even more there.

### L85. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/results-parsing-and-metrics/references/workflows.md:17`  
*skills:results-parsing-and-metrics · other · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "Both import the vendored eval utilities (`medvision_utils`), which import `torch`, `transformers` and the sibling package `medvision_ds` at module level, so all three must be installed" (same list in SKILL.md:15-16, api-reference.md:66-69 "every `medvision_bm.benchmark.parse_outputs` / `summarize_*` run, which import it", and the ModuleNotFoundError row of troubleshooting.md:105).
- **Code says:** summarize_detection_task.py:13-40 imports only argparse/glob/json/multiprocessing/os/re/collections/functools/numpy plus medvision_bm.utils.configs and medvision_bm.utils.parse_utils — it never imports medvision_utils, so `transformers` is not needed for step 3 on Detection. Conversely the list omits the HF `datasets` package, which every one of these commands pulls in at module level: medvision_bm/utils/__init__.py:1 `from .data_utils import *` -> data_utils.py:1 `from datasets import load_dataset` (and utils/utils.py:5 `import torch`). Verified: `PYTHONPATH=src python -c "from medvision_bm.utils.configs import SEED"` fails with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'datasets'`. `datasets==3.6.0` and `torch==2.6.0` are pyproject.toml:12-22 dependencies, but `transformers` is not declared there at all (it only appears in requirements/requirements_eval_*.txt).
- **Fix:** List the real import set: torch + HF `datasets` (via medvision_bm.utils.__init__) for all three summarizers and parse_outputs; `transformers` + `medvision_ds` additionally at module level for parse_outputs / summarize_TL / summarize_AD (which import medvision_utils), and medvision_ds at call time for label maps in all three. Add `No module named 'datasets'` to the troubleshooting row and note that `transformers` is not a declared dependency of medvision_bm.

### L86. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/results-parsing-and-metrics/references/workflows.md:60`  
*skills:results-parsing-and-metrics · coverage-gap · confirmed*

- **Claim:** A/D section: "Groups records by `"<dataset>_<metric_type>_<metric_key>"` (for example `Ceph-Biometrics-400_angle_SNA`, `FeTA24_distance_L-1-2`); Ceph files are summarized one file per key, all `FeTA24_BiometricsFromLandmarks_Task01*` files (three planes) are combined." Nothing says what happens to any other JSONL in the folder.

- **Fix:** Add one sentence (workflows.md A/D section and/or troubleshooting.md): the A/D summarizer hard-codes the two dataset name patterns in `find_and_group_jsonl_files`; JSONLs from any other dataset/task are silently ignored, so adding an A/D dataset requires editing that function.

### L87. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/rft/references/workflows.md:19`  
*skills:rft · wrong-name · confirmed*

- **Claim:** A0 §4: SFT-namespace task lists have "keys like `<dataset>_BoxSize_Task01_axial`" (lowercase plane token).

The key is used verbatim to build the HF config name (src/medvision_bm/sft/sft_utils.py:2191 `task + "_Train"`), and config names are case-sensitive (README.md:1374 `config = "OAIZIB-CM_BoxSize_Task01_Axial_Test"`), so a hand-written lowercase key would fail to load. The sibling sub-skill writes it correctly (skills/medvision/sub-skills/dataset-and-tasks/references/task-lists.md:9).
- **Fix:** Write the example as `<dataset>_BoxSize_Task01_Axial` (capital plane token).

- **Code says:** The A/D portion is not axial-only. tasks_list/tasks_MedVision-AD__train_SFT.json (the list the A/D parquet builder reads, per script/rft/build_parquet_ds__verl__D0k-AD5.5k-TL0k__512x512.sh:42) contains 4 non-axial configs: `Ceph-Biometrics-400_BiometricsFromLandmarks_Distance_Task01_Sagittal`, `..._Angle_Task01_Sagittal`, `FeTA24_BiometricsFromLandmarks_Task01_Sagittal`, `..._Task01_Coronal`, plus one `..._Task01_Axial`. README.md:861 states it explicitly: "Detection and T/L only, since A/D has no plane-OOD split." (Only the detect and TL SFT lists are 100% `_Axial`.) The blanket claim is inherited from paper.tex:207, which is equally imprecise.
